I don't really get much out of things anymore. Like I mentioned with the fishing and the gardening... it's just not the same. I mostly just do them because I feel like I'm supposed to, not because I actually enjoy them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I feel guilty about things, no more than anyone else, anyway. I mean, I haven't done anything wrong, I'm just... tired and flat. But no, guilt isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been quite a while. I can actually pinpoint it pretty well—it really started right around the time I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was... about two years and four months ago. It's just been going on steadily ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel particularly anxious or worried about anything. It's more just... a lack of energy and interest, like I said. But I'm not sitting around worrying or feeling on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than normal. I'm just tired, but I'm still functioning at my usual pace, I guess. Nobody else has mentioned it to me either.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. My appetite has been off more days than not lately. I'll just... I'll work right through lunch without even noticing, and then I just kind of pick at my dinner. My wife actually pointed out recently that my pants are fitting a lot looser, even though I'm definitely not trying to lose weight. It's just that I'm not eating as much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It's not exactly a crying kind of sad, though. For a long time now, there's just been this... well, this gray, flat feeling most days. Even the things I used to really look forward to—like going fishing, tending to the tomatoes out back, or going to a church concert—they don't really do much for me anymore. I don't think I feel guilty about things, no more than anyone else, anyway. I mean, I haven't done anything wrong, I'm just... tired and flat. But no, guilt isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been quite a while. I can actually pinpoint it pretty well—it really started right around the time I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was... about two years and four months ago. It's just been going on steadily ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel particularly anxious or worried about anything. It's more just... a lack of energy and interest, like I said. But I'm not sitting around worrying or feeling on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than normal. I'm just tired, but I'm still functioning at my usual pace, I guess. Nobody else has mentioned it to me either.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. My appetite has been off more days than not lately. I'll just... I'll work right through lunch without even noticing, and then I just kind of pick at my dinner. My wife actually pointed out recently that my pants are fitting a lot looser, even though I'm definitely not trying to lose weight. It's just that I'm not eating as much.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't had any thoughts about wanting to die or... disappearing. I mean, I'm definitely feeling drained and just kind of down, but I'm not looking to end things or anything extreme like that. I still want to be here.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My focus at work is fine, and I haven't had any trouble making decisions or anything like that. I'm just tired, but my mind is still sharp.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this gray, flat feeling is something that affects you most of the day, or is it more intermittent?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's most of the day, yeah. Really, it's been like this most days for a long time now. It's just this constant gray, flat feeling that doesn't really lift. \n\nEven the things I used to really look forward to... you know, going fishing, tending to the tomatoes in the garden, or heading to a church concert... they don't bring me much joy anymore. I still do them, but they just feel like items on a checklist instead of something I actually enjoy doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are any days where you feel somewhat better, or is the gray feeling consistently the same every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't really noticed any days where it lifts, to be honest. It's... I guess it's pretty consistent. I mean, I'm not keeping a chart or anything, but looking back, it all just kind of blends together into that same flat feeling. I don't really have days where I wake up and feel, you know, back to normal. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or doing nothing more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. They just feel like items on a checklist to get through, instead of something I actually enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, it's definitely changed. I don't really get much out of things anymore. Like I mentioned with the fishing and the gardening... it's just not the same. I mostly just do them because I feel like I'm supposed to, not because I actually enjoy them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I feel guilty about things, no more than anyone else, anyway. I mean, I haven't done anything wrong, I'm just... tired and flat. But no, guilt isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been quite a while. I can actually pinpoint it pretty well—it really started right around the time I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was... about two years and four months ago. It's just been going on steadily ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel particularly anxious or worried about anything. It's more just... a lack of energy and interest, like I said. But I'm not sitting around worrying or feeling on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than normal. I'm just tired, but I'm still functioning at my usual pace, I guess. Nobody else has mentioned it to me either.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. My appetite has been off more days than not lately. I'll just... I'll work right through lunch without even noticing, and then I just kind of pick at my dinner. My wife actually pointed out recently that my pants are fitting a lot looser, even though I'm definitely not trying to lose weight. It's just that I'm not eating as much.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't had any thoughts about wanting to die or... disappearing. I mean, I'm definitely feeling drained and just kind of down, but I'm not looking to end things or anything extreme like that. I still want to be here.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My focus at work is fine, and I haven't had any trouble making decisions or anything like that. I'm just tired, but my mind is still sharp.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this gray, flat feeling is something that affects you most of the day, or is it more intermittent?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's most of the day, yeah. Really, it's been like this most days for a long time now. It's just this constant gray, flat feeling that doesn't really lift. \n\nEven the things I used to really look forward to... you know, going fishing, tending to the tomatoes in the garden, or heading to a church concert... they don't bring me much joy anymore. I still do them, but they just feel like items on a checklist instead of something I actually enjoy doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are any days where you feel somewhat better, or is the gray feeling consistently the same every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't really noticed any days where it lifts, to be honest. It's... I guess it's pretty consistent. I mean, I'm not keeping a chart or anything, but looking back, it all just kind of blends together into that same flat feeling. I don't really have days where I wake up and feel, you know, back to normal. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or doing nothing more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I'm still working my regular hours managing the IT team, and I keep up with my routines at home. Like I mentioned, I still do the gardening and everything else... I just don't get much out of it. So, I wouldn't say I'm resting more or just sitting around doing nothing. I'm still as active as I usually am, I just feel flat while I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have noticed any difference in the way you move or carry yourself lately?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. Nobody's mentioned anything to me about moving differently or, you know, walking slower or anything like that. I feel like I'm moving around the same as I always do.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly I came in because I’m tired in a way that just doesn’t really make sense. I mean, I sleep fine, and I keep up with my work just fine, but I still feel like... well, like I’m running on a low battery before the day even really gets going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose you could say that. It's not exactly a crying kind of sad, though. For a long time now, there's just been this... well, this gray, flat feeling most days. Even the things I used to really look forward to—like going fishing, tending to the tomatoes out back, or going to a church concert—they don't really do much for me anymore. They just feel like items on a checklist to get through, instead of something I actually enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, it's definitely changed. I don't really get much out of things anymore. Like I mentioned with the fishing and the gardening... it's just not the same. I mostly just do them because I feel like I'm supposed to, not because I actually enjoy them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I feel guilty about things, no more than anyone else, anyway. I mean, I haven't done anything wrong, I'm just... tired and flat. But no, guilt isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been quite a while. I can actually pinpoint it pretty well—it really started right around the time I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was... about two years and four months ago. It's just been going on steadily ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel particularly anxious or worried about anything. It's more just... a lack of energy and interest, like I said. But I'm not sitting around worrying or feeling on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than normal. I'm just tired, but I'm still functioning at my usual pace, I guess. Nobody else has mentioned it to me either.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. My appetite has been off more days than not lately. I'll just... I'll work right through lunch without even noticing, and then I just kind of pick at my dinner. My wife actually pointed out recently that my pants are fitting a lot looser, even though I'm definitely not trying to lose weight. It's just that I'm not eating as much.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't had any thoughts about wanting to die or... disappearing. I mean, I'm definitely feeling drained and just kind of down, but I'm not looking to end things or anything extreme like that. I still want to be here.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My focus at work is fine, and I haven't had any trouble making decisions or anything like that. I'm just tired, but my mind is still sharp.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this gray, flat feeling is something that affects you most of the day, or is it more intermittent?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's most of the day, yeah. Really, it's been like this most days for a long time now. It's just this constant gray, flat feeling that doesn't really lift. \n\nEven the things I used to really look forward to... you know, going fishing, tending to the tomatoes in the garden, or heading to a church concert... they don't bring me much joy anymore. I still do them, but they just feel like items on a checklist instead of something I actually enjoy doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are any days where you feel somewhat better, or is the gray feeling consistently the same every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't really noticed any days where it lifts, to be honest. It's... I guess it's pretty consistent. I mean, I'm not keeping a chart or anything, but looking back, it all just kind of blends together into that same flat feeling. I don't really have days where I wake up and feel, you know, back to normal. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or doing nothing more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I'm still working my regular hours managing the IT team, and I keep up with my routines at home. Like I mentioned, I still do the gardening and everything else... I just don't get much out of it. So, I wouldn't say I'm resting more or just sitting around doing nothing. I'm still as active as I usually am, I just feel flat while I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have noticed any difference in the way you move or carry yourself lately?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. Nobody's mentioned anything to me about moving differently or, you know, walking slower or anything like that. I feel like I'm moving around the same as I always do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're fidgeting or restless more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel restless or fidgety. I'm pretty much just going about my day at my normal pace. Nothing like that has really changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel that way. I know I'm capable, I do my job well, and I take care of things at home. It's really just that flat feeling I mentioned. But no, I wouldn't say I feel worthless at all.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current feelings as being mildly bothersome or significantly impacting your daily life?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's somewhere in between. I mean, I'm still getting the essential work done at my job, so I'm functioning there. But... well, I have missed some routine appointments lately. And I've pretty much just let the garden go. I've also backed out of some church and community things that I used to be really reliable for. So, the main things are getting done, but it's definitely affecting the rest of my life more than just a mild bother.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

They just feel like items on a checklist to get through, instead of something I actually enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, it's definitely changed. I don't really get much out of things anymore. Like I mentioned with the fishing and the gardening... it's just not the same. I mostly just do them because I feel like I'm supposed to, not because I actually enjoy them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I feel guilty about things, no more than anyone else, anyway. I mean, I haven't done anything wrong, I'm just... tired and flat. But no, guilt isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been quite a while. I can actually pinpoint it pretty well—it really started right around the time I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was... about two years and four months ago. It's just been going on steadily ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel particularly anxious or worried about anything. It's more just... a lack of energy and interest, like I said. But I'm not sitting around worrying or feeling on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than normal. I'm just tired, but I'm still functioning at my usual pace, I guess. Nobody else has mentioned it to me either.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. My appetite has been off more days than not lately. I'll just... I'll work right through lunch without even noticing, and then I just kind of pick at my dinner. My wife actually pointed out recently that my pants are fitting a lot looser, even though I'm definitely not trying to lose weight. It's just that I'm not eating as much.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't had any thoughts about wanting to die or... disappearing. I mean, I'm definitely feeling drained and just kind of down, but I'm not looking to end things or anything extreme like that. I still want to be here.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My focus at work is fine, and I haven't had any trouble making decisions or anything like that. I'm just tired, but my mind is still sharp.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this gray, flat feeling is something that affects you most of the day, or is it more intermittent?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's most of the day, yeah. Really, it's been like this most days for a long time now. It's just this constant gray, flat feeling that doesn't really lift. \n\nEven the things I used to really look forward to... you know, going fishing, tending to the tomatoes in the garden, or heading to a church concert... they don't bring me much joy anymore. I still do them, but they just feel like items on a checklist instead of something I actually enjoy doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are any days where you feel somewhat better, or is the gray feeling consistently the same every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't really noticed any days where it lifts, to be honest. It's... I guess it's pretty consistent. I mean, I'm not keeping a chart or anything, but looking back, it all just kind of blends together into that same flat feeling. I don't really have days where I wake up and feel, you know, back to normal. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or doing nothing more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I'm still working my regular hours managing the IT team, and I keep up with my routines at home. Like I mentioned, I still do the gardening and everything else... I just don't get much out of it. So, I wouldn't say I'm resting more or just sitting around doing nothing. I'm still as active as I usually am, I just feel flat while I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have noticed any difference in the way you move or carry yourself lately?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. Nobody's mentioned anything to me about moving differently or, you know, walking slower or anything like that. I feel like I'm moving around the same as I always do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're fidgeting or restless more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel restless or fidgety. I'm pretty much just going about my day at my normal pace. Nothing like that has really changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel that way. I know I'm capable, I do my job well, and I take care of things at home. It's really just that flat feeling I mentioned. But no, I wouldn't say I feel worthless at all.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current feelings as being mildly bothersome or significantly impacting your daily life?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's somewhere in between. I mean, I'm still getting the essential work done at my job, so I'm functioning there. But... well, I have missed some routine appointments lately. And I've pretty much just let the garden go. I've also backed out of some church and community things that I used to be really reliable for. So, the main things are getting done, but it's definitely affecting the rest of my life more than just a mild bother.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this flat feeling is severe enough that it's significantly impacting your daily life, or is it more manageable?\n\nPatient: I think I just answered that, really. It's... it's somewhere in between. Like I said, I'm getting my main work done, but I'm dropping the ball on the extra stuff—the garden, the community things, my appointments. It's manageable enough that I'm still working, but it's definitely impacting my life more than just a little bit.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this flat feeling is making it harder for you to work or take care of things at home most of the time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's not really stopping me from doing the absolute necessities. I'm still managing my job and keeping the house running. But it's the other stuff that's sliding. I've missed a few appointments lately, and my garden is just completely overgrown right now. I've also stopped helping out with some church activities that I used to handle. So, the main things are okay, but I'm definitely letting the rest of it go.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I can actually pinpoint it pretty well—it really started right around the time I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was... about two years and four months ago. It's just been going on steadily ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel particularly anxious or worried about anything. It's more just... a lack of energy and interest, like I said. But I'm not sitting around worrying or feeling on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than normal. I'm just tired, but I'm still functioning at my usual pace, I guess. Nobody else has mentioned it to me either.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. My appetite has been off more days than not lately. I'll just... I'll work right through lunch without even noticing, and then I just kind of pick at my dinner. My wife actually pointed out recently that my pants are fitting a lot looser, even though I'm definitely not trying to lose weight. It's just that I'm not eating as much.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't had any thoughts about wanting to die or... disappearing. I mean, I'm definitely feeling drained and just kind of down, but I'm not looking to end things or anything extreme like that. I still want to be here.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My focus at work is fine, and I haven't had any trouble making decisions or anything like that. I'm just tired, but my mind is still sharp.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this gray, flat feeling is something that affects you most of the day, or is it more intermittent?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's most of the day, yeah. Really, it's been like this most days for a long time now. It's just this constant gray, flat feeling that doesn't really lift. \n\nEven the things I used to really look forward to... you know, going fishing, tending to the tomatoes in the garden, or heading to a church concert... they don't bring me much joy anymore. I still do them, but they just feel like items on a checklist instead of something I actually enjoy doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are any days where you feel somewhat better, or is the gray feeling consistently the same every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't really noticed any days where it lifts, to be honest. It's... I guess it's pretty consistent. I mean, I'm not keeping a chart or anything, but looking back, it all just kind of blends together into that same flat feeling. I don't really have days where I wake up and feel, you know, back to normal. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or doing nothing more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I'm still working my regular hours managing the IT team, and I keep up with my routines at home. Like I mentioned, I still do the gardening and everything else... I just don't get much out of it. So, I wouldn't say I'm resting more or just sitting around doing nothing. I'm still as active as I usually am, I just feel flat while I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people have noticed any difference in the way you move or carry yourself lately?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. Nobody's mentioned anything to me about moving differently or, you know, walking slower or anything like that. I feel like I'm moving around the same as I always do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're fidgeting or restless more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel restless or fidgety. I'm pretty much just going about my day at my normal pace. Nothing like that has really changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel that way. I know I'm capable, I do my job well, and I take care of things at home. It's really just that flat feeling I mentioned. But no, I wouldn't say I feel worthless at all.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current feelings as being mildly bothersome or significantly impacting your daily life?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's somewhere in between. I mean, I'm still getting the essential work done at my job, so I'm functioning there. But... well, I have missed some routine appointments lately. And I've pretty much just let the garden go. I've also backed out of some church and community things that I used to be really reliable for. So, the main things are getting done, but it's definitely affecting the rest of my life more than just a mild bother.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this flat feeling is severe enough that it's significantly impacting your daily life, or is it more manageable?\n\nPatient: I think I just answered that, really. It's... it's somewhere in between. Like I said, I'm getting my main work done, but I'm dropping the ball on the extra stuff—the garden, the community things, my appointments. It's manageable enough that I'm still working, but it's definitely impacting my life more than just a little bit.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this flat feeling is making it harder for you to work or take care of things at home most of the time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's not really stopping me from doing the absolute necessities. I'm still managing my job and keeping the house running. But it's the other stuff that's sliding. I've missed a few appointments lately, and my garden is just completely overgrown right now. I've also stopped helping out with some church activities that I used to handle. So, the main things are okay, but I'm definitely letting the rest of it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't been weighing myself, but my wife actually pointed out recently that my pants are looking a lot looser. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything. It's just... my appetite has been off more days than not. I'll get caught up with work and just go right through lunch without even noticing I missed it, and then I mostly just pick at my dinner.
